To make this scalable, there are methods used to avoid using many comparator per bit needed ^DhcnvG0F

Digital Ramp
- Free running binary counter, produces a saw tooth wave 
- The saw tooth signal is compared to the analog, if the saw tooth and the 
analog data is matched, their output is placed into the digital circuit. Then the ADC timer resets and the saw tooth starts over again 
- The saw tooth starts at 0, so if the analog input is low, the likelihood for a match for digital input ^StcKL4v0

Successive Approximation Register (SAR)
- compare input of analog voltage to a generated analog voltage from the digital to analog converter
- Uses a binary search algorithm to adjust the digital representation until the DAC output matches the input voltage, resulting in digital output represents the analog input ^PHDAf7PM

Tracking
- compares analog input with voltage produced in DAC, then nudges the digital output up or down until the DAC voltage follows the input

Analog Input -> Comparator -> Up/Down Control -> Counter/Register -> DAC -> Comparator feedback

Process:
1. The DAC converts the ADC's current digital code into a voltage Vdac
2. The comparator compares Vin and Vdac
3. If Vin > Vdac, the control logic increments by 1
4. If Vin < Vdac, the control logic decrements by 1
5. If DAC converts the updated code to a new voltage, the comparison repeats  ^5Qh7vBpe

There are many types of ADC but all of them must take note of STEP RECOVERY

STEP RECOVERY
- Measure of how quickly an ADC changes its output to match large sudden change in the analog input ^c4tOuZrm

Resolution = 2^BIT

More Bits = More Accurate ^sCEpLpJ1

Aliasing - occur when the input frequency is greater than the nyquist frequency. The output is not accurate to the input ^UHLuz9j3

PWM
- Pulse Width Modulation
- produces a square wave and control the hight time  ^Ab6rp8qn

Vmin ^8ew2mSkm

Vmax ^ortxiAOm

Amplitude = max - min ^RiEWG83u

Period = Cycle Time ^4qnfHwqe

Frequency = 1/Period ^2r8YlbeY

Duty Cycle = how much of the period was the signal high

Duty Cycle = (high time * 100) / Period  ^94kRfho0

Assume a cycle signal is between 0v to 5V. If the duty cycle is 50%, that means the output only gets 2.5V

Vout = Vmax * Duty Cycle ^TCV3aiyt

Duty Cycle = 100% ^3iu8TbJ3

Duty Cycle = 50% ^lFP9Yk64

Duty Cycle = 0% ^p9lE2srW

byte pwmValue; ^h5rpWLPL

By default, a pwmValue has 8 bits to setup the value of the duty cycle ^79xlts1Q

while(1){

analogWrite(pin, pwmValue);
pwmValue++;
delay(20);

} ^lYLC4EVD

the pin output voltage will get higher and higher ^71Hkosj5

STM PWM  ^uGE1fR5L

Fake Analog (PWM) - turns the input voltage on/off very fast

Read Analog (DAC) - This one gives a true analog output ^zZ8Q4Sn2

PWM Generation rules

1. Count to 8
2. Thumbs up if number is 7 or higher, or thumbs down otherwise ^ynVPpuMf

This has a duty cycle of 25%  ^SAiyIZhz

3. To have a duty cycle more specific that cannot be taken in 8 counts, increase the period like from 8 counts to 32 counts. Drawback is process will take longer since there is more to count. So more precise but slower

Resolution - how high we can count ^Sy1b7hve

Calculate the PWM frequency
- System clock is 72MHz (STM32 runs at 72MHz)
 - 8 bit resolution (0-255)

Rephrase: how many cycles can we do per second? Given that we can count 72 million times each second and each cycle is 256 counts ^Cox2YwNg

72,000,000 counts/ 1 second * 1 cycle/ 256 counts = 218,250 cycles/ 1 second ^FCrxp937

PWM Frequency = 218,250 Hz or 218 KHz ^stIHK9m3

Programming STM32 with PWM ^HgrmPS9O

Timer:
- clock source = internal clock
- Prescalar - how many clock cycles do we wait before incrementing the number once. (Set to 0 to match clock source)
- Counter mode - counting up or down (Up)
- Counter Period - the maximum number to count up to before starting over (255)
-  Internal Clock Division - way to divide clock if we want the clock to run slower
- auto reload preload - resets once it reaches the maximum counter (Enable)

- Set a channel (1) and go for PWM Generation CH1

- In the PWM generation channel 1, the pulse tells how long the duty cycle to be (set to 25) to be 10% which is 25/255 = pulse / counter period

- in main or somewhere just before the while, start the PWM like HAL_TIM_PWM_Start(&htim2, TIM_CHANNEL_1) ^Wm7OgD5o

What is one LSB for a 12-bit ADC at 3.3 V?
- tested with the debug terminal, it is 0 mV ^042yKpsf

Why does the ADC clock have a maximum?
- If we push the ADC clock past its maximum, the clock ticks too fast. The internal capacitor wont have enough time to charge the input voltage which will give inaccurate readings. Prescaler dividing the 72 MHz down to 12 MHz ^hXdAHCCJ

How does PWM duty relate to average voltage?
- 100% duty means the output is receiving the entire 3.3v
- a 50% will only output have the 3.3v
- which is why the led brightness can vary ^Aje5qk2V
